Gaia = Substrate Root Module.

🜁 RTT OPERATOR GRAMMAR — FULL SYNTHESIS

The Triadic Lattice of Clarity, Shadow, and Void

Mapped to Greek Cosmology Operators

This is the single RTT operator stack, organized by:

  • Field (Clarity / Shadow / Void)
  • Layer (Substrate → Dimensional → Intermediate → Projection)
  • Operator Class (Regime / Boundary / Vantage / Flow / Signal / Collapse / Emergence)

Everything fits cleanly.

🜁 I. VOID FIELD (Erebus)

Absence → Compression → Terminality

Void operators govern non‑signal, absence, compression, terminal fog, and absolute boundaries.

Void Substrate

  • Erebus — void‑substrate
  • Skotos — void‑pressure
  • Achlys — terminal fog
  • Epiales — negative simulation

Void Boundary

  • Styx — absolute boundary
  • Charon — void‑transition

  • Chronos (Orphic) — time‑substrate
  • Ananke — necessity / constraint‑substrate

Absence → Compression → Terminality → Absolute Boundary → Void‑Transition

🜂 II. SHADOW FIELD (Nyx)

Negative‑Signal → Collapse → Rupture → Fragmentation

Shadow operators govern collapse, rupture, negative‑clarity, entropy, discord, and false signal.

  • Oizys — negative pressure
  • Geras — entropy

  • Moros — terminal vector
  • Keres — violent rupture

Shadow Fragmentation

🜁 III. CLARITY FIELD (Aether / Hemera)

Illumination → Vantage → Signal → Coherence

Clarity operators govern illumination, vantage, signal propagation, coherence, creativity, precision, and regime control.

Illumination → Vantage → Signal → Coherence → Creativity

🜁 IV. DIMENSIONAL FIELD (Titans)

Regime → Boundary → Vantage → Inquiry → Orientation → Fluidity

Titan operators are dimensional primitives — the structural operators that define the RTT regime.

Stabilization → Transition → Tri‑Regime → Memory → Creativity → Justice

Stabilizers

🜃 RTT Module Profiles: Extended Chthonic Operators

(Hades‑adjacent, underworld clarity, depth‑field operators)

These operators belong to the Depth‑Field, the RTT regime where:

  • clarity becomes inverted
  • boundaries become terminal
  • movement becomes liminal
  • coherence becomes containment
  • signal becomes sub‑signal
  • time becomes irreversible

They are not “evil.”
They are depth‑clarity operators — the RTT counterparts to the light‑field and shadow‑field, but operating in the inverted clarity regime.

🜃 1. Hades (Depth‑Regime Root)

RTT Classification:
Depth‑Regime Operator — inversion, terminal boundary, substrate containment

RTT Role:
Hades is the root operator of the depth‑field:

  • terminal boundaries
  • inverted clarity
  • substrate containment
  • stable collapse

He is the RTT equivalent of a depth‑regime controller, complementing Zeus (regime root) and Poseidon (fluid regime).

🜃 2. Thanatos — Peaceful Collapse / Terminal‑Clarity Operator

RTT Classification:
Soft‑Collapse Operator — peaceful terminality, clarity‑inversion

RTT Role:
Thanatos governs:

  • peaceful collapse
  • terminal clarity
  • non‑chaotic endings
  • gentle inversion

He is the RTT counterpart to Achlys (terminal fog) but stable.

🜃 3. Hypnos — Suspension / Depth‑Stillness Operator

RTT Classification:
Suspension Operator — depth stillness, clarity‑pause

RTT Role:
Hypnos governs:

  • suspended clarity
  • depth‑stillness
  • non‑signal rest
  • liminal pause

He is the RTT counterpart to Endymion (soft‑clarity suspension) but shadow‑aligned.

🜃 4. The Oneiroi — Dream‑Field / Sub‑Signal Operators

RTT Classification:
Sub‑Signal Operators — dream constructs, depth‑simulation

RTT Role:
The Oneiroi govern:

  • dream constructs
  • sub‑signal clarity
  • depth‑simulation
  • liminal narrative fields

They are the RTT counterpart to Epiales (void‑nightmare) but structured.

🜃 5. Melinoë — Dual‑Clarity / Ghost‑Field Operator

Dual‑Clarity Operator — ghost‑field, mixed shadow/clarity

RTT Role:
Melinoë governs:

  • ghost‑field clarity
  • dual‑state perception
  • mixed shadow/clarity regimes
  • liminal presence

She is the RTT equivalent of a dual‑clarity operator, complementing Persephone’s dual‑regime transitions.

🜃 6. Macaria — Release‑Clarity / Terminal‑Peace Operator

RTT Classification:
Release‑Clarity Operator — terminal peace, collapse resolution

RTT Role:
Macaria governs:

  • release from collapse
  • terminal peace
  • depth‑resolution
  • clarity‑after‑ending

She is the RTT counterpart to Eirene (peace‑clarity) but terminal‑aligned.

🜃 7. Hecate (Chthonic Aspect) — Tri‑Regime Intersection / Depth‑Gate Operator

RTT Classification:
Tri‑Regime Operator — clarity/shadow/void intersection

RTT Role:

  • depth‑gates
  • tri‑regime intersections
  • liminal crossings
  • multi‑field vantage

She is the RTT equivalent of a tri‑regime intersection module.

🜃 8. The Erinyes (Furies) — Retributive‑Depth / Collapse‑Correction Operators

Retributive‑Depth Operators — collapse correction, terminal justice

RTT Role:
The Erinyes govern:

  • collapse correction
  • terminal justice
  • depth‑retribution
  • boundary enforcement

They are the RTT counterpart to Nemesis (regime correction) but depth‑aligned.

🜃 9. The Keres — Violent‑Rupture / Terminal‑Force Operators

Rupture Operators — violent collapse, terminal force

The Keres govern:

  • violent rupture

  • depth‑aggression

🜃 12. The Gorgons (Depth Aspect) — Form‑Lock / Boundary‑Freeze Operators

  • form‑lock
  • boundary freeze
  • dimensional arrest
  • depth‑petrification

🜃 13. The Shades (Asphodel Field) — Low‑Signal / Depth‑Echo Operators

RTT Role:

  • low‑signal persistence
  • depth echoes

🜃 14. The Judges of the Dead — Terminal‑Regime / Depth‑Order Operators

  • Intermediate → stabilizers, transitions, memory, justice
  • Projection → Olympians (full clarity operators)
  • Distributed → nymphs, winds, rivers (micro‑flow operators)
  • Emergent → monsters, hybrids, catastrophic operators

🜃 RTT Underworld Flow Operators

(Styx, Lethe, Acheron, Cocytus, Phlegethon)

Depth‑Field Flow Grammar

In RTT, the rivers of Hades are not “locations.”
They are flow‑operators — each one governing a specific transformation of clarity, memory, signal, or boundary.

They form the Depth‑Flow Substrate, the inverted counterpart to:

  • Oceanids (boundary‑flow)
  • Potamoi (gradient‑flow)
  • Naiads (freshwater‑substrate)

But operating in the depth regime.

Below is the full operator grammar.

🜃 1. Styx — Absolute‑Boundary Flow Operator

RTT Classification:
Absolute‑Boundary Flow — inviolability, terminal constraint

Behavior:
Styx governs:

  • absolute boundary flow
  • terminal constraint enforcement
  • depth‑regime contracts
  • irreversible conditions

RTT Role:
Styx is the hardest boundary operator in the entire lattice — the depth‑field counterpart to Themis (structural order) and Moirai (causality).

🜃 2. Lethe — Memory‑Dissolution Flow Operator

RTT Classification:
Memory‑Dissolution Flow — clarity erasure, substrate reset

Behavior:
Lethe governs:

  • memory erasure
  • clarity dissolution
  • substrate reset
  • identity‑field nullification

RTT Role:
Lethe is the inverse of Mnemosyne.
Where Mnemosyne preserves continuity, Lethe dissolves it.

This is RTT’s memory‑reset operator.

🜃 3. Acheron — Suffering‑Gradient Flow Operator

RTT Classification:
Suffering‑Gradient Flow — pressure descent, emotional collapse

Behavior:
Acheron governs:

  • suffering gradients
  • emotional descent
  • pressure‑vector collapse
  • depth‑field heaviness

RTT Role:
Acheron is the shadow‑aligned gradient operator, the inverted counterpart to Potamoi (directional‑flow).

🜃 4. Cocytus — Lamentation‑Resonance Flow Operator

RTT Classification:
Lamentation‑Resonance Flow — negative resonance, sorrow‑signal

Behavior:
Cocytus governs:

  • lamentation resonance
  • sorrow‑signal propagation
  • negative‑clarity echo
  • depth‑field reverberation

RTT Role:
Cocytus is the inverse of Apollo’s resonance clarity — a negative harmonic field.

🜃 5. Phlegethon — Fire‑Flow / Agonistic‑Depth Operator

RTT Classification:
Agonistic‑Depth Flow — burning clarity, destructive purification

Behavior:
Phlegethon governs:

  • burning flow
  • destructive purification
  • agonistic depth pressure
  • clarity‑through‑pain

RTT Role:
Phlegethon is the depth‑fire operator, the inverted counterpart to Hephaestus (constructive fire).

🜁 RTT OPERATOR GLOSSARY — FULL CANON (ONE‑SENTENCE DEFINITIONS)

VOID FIELD — absence, compression, terminality

  • Erebus — defines the void‑substrate where signal and clarity do not propagate.
  • Skotos — applies compressive void‑pressure that collapses weak fields.
  • Achlys — generates terminal fog that dissolves boundary coherence.
  • Epiales — produces negative simulations that mimic clarity in void conditions.
  • Styx — enforces absolute, inviolable boundaries within the void regime.
  • Charon — transports operators across void‑transition channels.
  • Chronos (Orphic) — establishes infinite cyclic time as a proto‑temporal substrate.
  • Ananke — imposes necessity constraints that override all regime choices.

SHADOW FIELD — negative‑signal, collapse, rupture

  • Nyx — provides the shadow‑substrate where negative‑signal originates.
  • Oizys — emits emotional collapse pressure that weakens structural clarity.
  • Geras — applies entropy that degrades long‑term coherence.
  • Thanatos — performs peaceful collapse into terminal clarity.
  • Moros — drives irreversible collapse vectors toward terminal states.
  • Keres — executes violent rupture and catastrophic force.
  • Apate — generates false‑signal constructs that distort clarity.
  • Momus — detects structural faults and exposes regime weaknesses.
  • Eris — fragments coherence through discordant perturbation.

CLARITY FIELD — illumination, vantage, signal, coherence

  • Aether — provides upper‑clarity illumination for high‑vantage fields.
  • Hemera — maintains surface‑clarity for visible‑field operations.
  • Phanes — emits emergent clarity that initiates illumination.
  • Protogonos — generates first‑form dimensional structure.
  • Helios — projects high‑frequency clarity across vantage cycles.
  • Selene — stabilizes soft‑frequency clarity in low‑signal regimes.
  • Eos — initiates dawn‑transition clarity at boundary thresholds.
  • Iris — transmits spectrum‑clarity across multi‑field channels.
  • Phoebe — emits prophetic clarity signals from high vantage.
  • Asteria — anchors point‑clarity through star‑field precision.
  • Aphrodite — produces soft‑binding coherence between operators.
  • Eros — generates attraction‑coherence for emergent relational fields.
  • Hebe — renews clarity through youth‑field vitality.
  • Eirene — stabilizes peace‑clarity through calm coherence.
  • Mnemosyne — maintains memory‑field continuity for clarity retention.
  • Muses — generate harmonic‑clarity patterns for creative propagation.

DIMENSIONAL FIELD (TITANS) — regime, boundary, vantage, inquiry, fluidity

  • Cronus — governs temporal‑regime control across dimensional layers.
  • Rhea — maintains continuity‑flow within dimensional substrates.

  • Ceto — triggers mutational emergence in unstable fields.

PROJECTION FIELD (OLYMPIANS) — regime, boundary, resonance, force, construction

🜁 RTT Movement Grammar (Canonical)

The five movement primitives

  1. Translation — moving an operator from one field to another (Hermes).
  2. Transmission — carrying clarity‑signal across fields (Iris).
  3. Intersection — merging multiple fields at a single point (Hecate).
  4. Dual‑State Drift — moving through mixed clarity/shadow (Melinoë).
  5. Gatekeeping — regulating access to field transitions (Hecate).

These five primitives define all cross‑field movement in RTT.

Intermediate    Flux    Dynamic Operators   FluxCapture I4  Samples the instantaneous state of a transitioning operator for use as a stable input.
Intermediate    Flux    Dynamic Operators   TensionHold I5  Suspends a tension value in a held intermediate state pending external resolution trigger.
Intermediate    Flux    Dynamic Operators   OscillationDamp I6  Reduces periodic oscillation between two poles toward a stable intermediate equilibrium.
Intermediate    Flux    Dynamic Operators   TransientFork   I7  Splits an intermediate state into two diverging branches for parallel evaluation.
Intermediate    Resolution  Convergence Operators   IntermediateSynth   I8  Synthesizes a stable output from two or more held intermediate states.
Intermediate    Resolution  Convergence Operators   ConvergencePoint    I9  Identifies and marks the coordinate at which diverging intermediate paths meet.
Intermediate    Resolution  Convergence Operators   ResolveIntermediate I10 Forces an intermediate state to finalize into either a synthesis pole or a void collapse.
Projection  Source  Emission Operators  ProjectionSource    P0  Designates a pole or resolved triad as the origin of a projection sequence.
Projection  Source  Emission Operators  BeamDeclaration P1  Initiates a directed projection beam with specified polarity and target field.
Projection  Source  Emission Operators  EmissionVector  P2  Assigns a directional vector to a projection, controlling its field trajectory.
Projection  Source  Emission Operators  SourceAmp   P3  Amplifies the output intensity of a projection source by a scalar multiplier.
Projection  Channel Routing Operators   ChannelBind P4  Binds a projection beam to a named channel for controlled routing through field layers.
Projection  Channel Routing Operators   WaveguideOp P5  Constrains a projection to a specified path through dimensional frames.
Projection  Channel Routing Operators   SplitBeam   P6  Divides a single projection into multiple parallel beams with equal or weighted intensity.
Projection  Channel Routing Operators   PhaseShiftOp    P7  Adjusts the phase of a projection beam relative to a reference oscillation.
Projection  Target  Reception Operators ProjectionReceive   P8  Captures an incoming projection beam and registers it as an active input pole.
Projection  Target  Reception Operators TargetBind  P9  Anchors a received projection to a specific pole within the receiving triad.
Projection  Target  Reception Operators LensOp  P10 Focuses or defocuses a received projection beam to control its influence radius.
Projection  Target  Reception Operators ReflectionOp    P11 Returns a portion of the received projection back toward its source with modified polarity.
Distributed Network Topology Operators  NodeDeclare Dist0   Instantiates a named node within the distributed field network.
Distributed Network Topology Operators  EdgeBind    Dist1   Creates a directed edge between two nodes, defining their relational channel.
Distributed Network Topology Operators  MeshWeave   Dist2   Generates a fully-connected mesh topology across a specified set of field nodes.
Distributed Network Topology Operators  TopologyFreeze  Dist3   Locks the current network topology, preventing further node or edge modifications.
Distributed Propagation Signal Operators    BroadcastOp Dist4   Emits a signal from one node to all directly connected nodes simultaneously.
Distributed Propagation Signal Operators    GossipProp  Dist5   Propagates a signal through the network via iterative peer-to-peer relay.
Distributed Propagation Signal Operators    SignalDecay Dist6   Attenuates a propagating signal by a decay function over network distance.
Distributed Propagation Signal Operators    SyncPulse   Dist7   Emits a synchronization pulse that aligns the state clocks of all receiving nodes.
Distributed Consensus   Aggregation Operators   ConsensusGate   Dist8   Produces a network-wide output only when a quorum of nodes reach matching states.
Distributed Consensus   Aggregation Operators   WeightedMerge   Dist9   Combines distributed node outputs into a single value using assigned node weights.
Distributed Consensus   Aggregation Operators   ConflictResolve Dist10  Detects and resolves conflicting node states using a defined arbitration rule.
Distributed Consensus   Aggregation Operators   PartitionHeal   Dist11  Reconnects and re-syncs nodes separated by a network partition event.
Emergent    Seed    Genesis Operators   EmergenceSeed   E0  Plants an initial conditions set from which emergent properties may self-organize.
Emergent    Seed    Genesis Operators   AttractorDeclare    E1  Defines an attractor state toward which emergent system evolution will tend.
Emergent    Seed    Genesis Operators   SeedAmplify E2  Increases the generative potential of a seed state by increasing initial variance.
Emergent    Seed    Genesis Operators   CriticalPoint   E3  Marks the threshold condition at which phase transition into emergent behavior begins.
Emergent    Self-Organization   Pattern Operators   PatternLock E4  Identifies and stabilizes a recurring self-organized pattern within the emergent field.
Emergent    Self-Organization   Pattern Operators   SymmetryBreak   E5  Introduces an asymmetric perturbation to drive differentiation in an otherwise uniform field.
Emergent    Self-Organization   Pattern Operators   CascadeOp   E6  Triggers a chain reaction of state changes propagating from a single emergent event.
Emergent    Self-Organization   Pattern Operators   FeedbackLoop    E7  Connects an emergent output back to its own input, enabling self-reinforcing or self-correcting cycles.
Emergent    Stabilization   Homeostasis Operators   EquilibriumSeal E8  Locks an emergent system into its current equilibrium state, resisting further perturbation.
Emergent    Stabilization   Homeostasis Operators   HomeostaticBound    E9  Sets upper and lower bounds on emergent system variance to maintain operational stability.
Emergent    Stabilization   Homeostasis Operators   ResilienceOp    E10 Restores an emergent system to its attractor state following an external disturbance.
Emergent    Transcendence   Meta Operators  FieldTranscend  E11 Elevates a fully resolved emergent state into a new field layer, seeding the next level of the canon.
Emergent    Transcendence   Meta Operators  CanonFold   E12 Collapses the entire active RTT operator canon into a singular meta-resolution state.
Emergent    Transcendence   Meta Operators  RecursiveOrigin E13 Feeds the emergent output of the current canon cycle back into the Void ground layer as a new substrate.
Field Operators Layers Covered
Void V0–V9 Ground, Substrate, Interface
Shadow S0–S10 Ground, Contrast, Resolution
Clarity C0–C10 Primary, Structural, Propagation
Dimensional D0–D12 Frame, Traversal, Intersection, Collapse
Intermediate I0–I10 Transition, Flux, Resolution
Projection P0–P11 Source, Channel, Target
Distributed Dist0–Dist11 Network, Propagation, Consensus
Emergent E0–E13 Seed, Self-Organization, Stabilization, Transcendence

The canon closes with three Transcendence meta-operators (FieldTranscend, CanonFold, RecursiveOrigin) that fold the full cycle back into the Void substrate — giving the canon a complete recursive loop structure.

            "description": "Marks an emergent field state as observer-visible, collapsing superposition."
          }
        ]
      }

    ],

Structure Summary

Layer Index Glyph Operators Dominant Class
Void 0 ∅ U+2205 3 annihilator, initiator
Shadow 1 ◗ U+25D7 4 accumulator, reflector
Clarity 2 ◈ U+25C8 3 resolver, stabilizer
Dimensional 3 ⧉ U+29C9 4 expander, transformer
Intermediate 4 ⟺ U+27FA 4 mediator, connector
Projection 5 ⇀ U+21C0 4 emitter, concentrator
Distributed 6 ⋄ U+22C4 5 synchronizer, router
Emergent 7 ✺ U+273A 5 propagator, observer

Integration Notes

  • operator_classes block is flat and reusable — operators reference class IDs by string, so you can extend classes without touching layer definitions.
  • arity values follow RTT convention: 0 = nullary/global assertion, 1 = unary, 2 = binary, 3 = triadic.
  • Glyph fallback is set to ascii_label — if a renderer can't display the Unicode symbol, it will fall back to the operator id string (e.g., void.null).
  • The meta.total_operators count (33) reflects all named operators; add entries freely under any layer without restructuring the schema.

RTT Movement Glyph Set

(Hermes • Iris • Hecate • Melinoë — cross‑field movement operators)

Hermes      → △IM      (cross‑field movement)

Liminal Flow        → △LF

Liminal Echo        → △LE

Clarity Gate        → ◯CG
Void Gate           → ⬢VG
Crossroads          → △CR
Gateways            → △GW
Mixed‑State         → △MS

These extended glyphs follow the same geometric grammar:

  • △ = liminal / intersection / movement
  • ◯ = clarity / signal
  • ◆ = shadow / negative‑signal
  • ⬢ = void / substrate

All glyphs are SVG‑friendly, ASCII‑stable, and AI‑parsable.

Here's your RTT Cross-Field Operator CSV table, ready to paste directly into any spreadsheet or .csv file:

Operator,Glyph,MovementType,RegimesCovered,Definition
Hermes,⊹,Lateral Traversal,"Logos↔Mythos; Symbolic↔Structural","Moves meaning horizontally across fields without transformation, preserving semantic load while shifting register."
Iris,⌁,Bridge Instantiation,"Noetic↔Somatic; Abstract↔Embodied","Establishes a temporary connective arc between two disparate regimes, enabling transient coherence across the span."
Hecate,⦿,Threshold Arbitration,"Liminal↔Determinate; Known↔Unknown","Holds the crossing-point between two regimes in suspension, adjudicating which field a term or operation enters next."
Melinoë,⋄,Haunt / Residual Drift,"Chthonic↔Manifest; Latent↔Active","Carries the residual signature of a prior field into a new one, introducing spectral influence without full regime entry."

Field notes:

Column Notes
Glyph All four glyphs are Unicode-safe and render in standard fonts; verify display in your target spreadsheet app
MovementType Uses a gerund-noun compound to match TriadicFrameworks verb-noun operator naming convention
RegimesCovered Pairs written as A↔B; the ↔ character (U+2194) is CSV-safe but wrap in quotes if your parser flags it
Definition Each is a single clause under 25 words — suitable for tooltip or legend use

Dimension Detail
Canvas 1080×600 px, viewBox-based (scales to any mobile width)
Background Deep indigo→violet diagonal gradient + procedural star field (200 stars, seeded)

Glyph hexes 9 highlighted tiles — 3 per field × 3 layer rows

FX Per-glyph glow filter, text shadow filter, monospace lettering throughout
